# Directional transience implies ballisticity

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0427` | `PM-220.3` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Directional-transience-implies-ballisticity-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint Directional-transience-implies-ballisticity-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0).
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that almost-sure transience in a fixed direction implies a deterministic limiting velocity with positive projection in that direction for nearest-neighbor random walks in independent and identically distributed uniformly elliptic environments on ℤ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, <i>d</i> ≥ 2. This resolves the ballisticity conjecture positively.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that almost-sure transience in a fixed direction implies a deterministic limiting velocity with positive projection in that direction for nearest-neighbor random walks in independent and identically distributed uniformly elliptic environments on ℤ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, <i>d</i> ≥ 2. This resolves the ballisticity conjecture positively.

- **claim-directional-transience-implies-ballisticity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $d\ge2$ and let $P=\nu^{\otimes\mathbb Z^d}$ be an iid law of nearest-neighbor transition rows satisfying uniform ellipticity. If $\ell\in S^{d-1}$ and $P_0(A_\ell)=1$, then there exists a deterministic vector $v=v(\nu)\in\mathbb R^d$ such that $$v\cdot\ell>0, \qquad P_0\!\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{X_n}{n}=v\right)=1.$$ In particular, the walk is ballistic in the same direction $\ell$.
  - Explanation: For nearest-neighbor walks in independent, identically distributed, uniformly elliptic environments in dimension at least two, almost-sure escape in one fixed direction forces a deterministic limiting velocity with strictly positive projection on that direction. In particular the walk is ballistic.
- **claim-positive-transience-velocity-hemisphere** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-positive-transience)): Let $d\ge3$ and let $P=\nu^{\otimes\mathbb Z^d}$ be an iid law of nearest-neighbor transition rows satisfying uniform ellipticity. For a fixed $\ell\in\mathbb R^d\setminus\{0\}$, set $A_\ell=\{X_n\cdot\ell\longrightarrow+\infty\}$. If $P_0(A_\ell)>0$, then there is a deterministic nonzero vector $v=v(\nu)$ such that $$v\cdot\ell>0, \qquad P_0\!\left(\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{X_n}{n}=v\right)=1.$$ If such a direction exists, then the deterministic sets of directions satisfy $$\{u\in S^{d-1}:P_0(A_u)=1\} =\{u\in S^{d-1}:P_0(A_u)>0\} =\{u\in S^{d-1}:v\cdot u>0\}.$$ In particular, $P_0(A_u)=0$ for each fixed $u\in S^{d-1}$ with $v\cdot u\le0$, including every equator direction. The equality concerns annealed probabilities for each fixed deterministic direction, not a simultaneous pathwise assertion over all directions.
  - Explanation: In dimension at least three, a positive probability of escape in a fixed direction already gives the deterministic velocity, via the companion zero-one law. The directions with positive escape probability are exactly the open hemisphere cut out by the velocity, for each fixed direction separately.
- **claim-true-words-iid** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-words)): We have $p_h>0$. Under $P_0(\,\cdot\mid D_h)$, include time zero as a true boundary. The relative finite path words between consecutive true boundaries are iid. The raw path has a finite first positive-time true record, after which its translated suffix has this conditioned law.* *For a conditioned word, let $L>0$ be its height gain, let $R$ be its maximal Euclidean distance from its starting point, including its endpoint, and let $S_*$ count its ordinary strict records after its start and through its end. Then $$0<\mathbb E L\le\mathbb E S_*\le p_h^{-1}.$$ Here and until the change to coordinate height below, word expectations refer to this conditioned word law.
  - Explanation: The non-backtracking probability is positive, and under the conditioned law the path splits into independent, identically distributed words between true boundaries. The mean height gain of a word is positive and bounded by the mean number of ordinary strict records, which is at most the reciprocal of that probability.
- **claim-integrable-spatial-radius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-radius)): Under the conditioned word law, $$\mathbb E R<\infty. \tag{1}\label{eq:1}$$ No moment of the word duration is assumed.
  - Explanation: The maximal distance a regeneration word reaches from its start has finite mean, without any moment assumption on the duration of the word.
- **claim-deterministic-limiting-ray** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-ray)): There is a deterministic unit vector $r$ with $h(r)>0$ such that $X_n/\\|X_n\\|\to r$ almost surely. After a deterministic signed permutation of the coordinate axes, $r_1>0$, and all the preceding word conclusions hold with height $x_1$.
  - Explanation: The walk has a deterministic asymptotic direction almost surely, with positive projection on the chosen height. After a signed permutation of coordinates this direction has positive first coordinate, and the word conclusions hold with the first coordinate as height.
- **claim-small-inverse-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-inverse)): For every site $x$, $a_H(x)\downarrow q(x)>0$ for almost every environment. Moreover, for some $\lambda>0$, $$E\,q(0)^{-\lambda}<\infty. \tag{4}\label{eq:4}$$
  - Explanation: Quenched crossing probabilities decrease to a positive quenched survival probability at every site, and the inverse of that survival probability has a finite small negative moment.
- **claim-crossing-estimate-gives-speed** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geom-speed)): If <a href="#eq:5" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:5">[eq:5]</a> holds, then $\mathbb E\tau_{\rm s}<\infty$, and the walk has an almost-sure deterministic velocity with strictly positive projection on the original direction $\ell$.
  - Explanation: Conditional on the super-polynomial crossing estimate, a single regeneration word has finite mean duration and the walk has an almost-sure deterministic velocity with positive projection on the original direction.
- **claim-common-words-fresh** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fluct-common-words)): Under either conditioned pair law, common true levels occur infinitely often. At each such level the translated pair continuation is fresh: under independent environments it has law $P_0^+\otimes P_0^+$, while under a shared environment, from terminal sites $x',y'$, it has law $P_{x',y'}^{++}$ before translation. The latter transition law depends on the terminal pair of sites.* *These continuation rules remain valid at finite stopping indices of the chain of common words, when the information retained consists of the traversed words and their path prefixes.
  - Explanation: Two walks, whether in independent or in a shared environment, have common true levels infinitely often. At each such level the pair continuation is fresh, with a law that in the shared case depends on the terminal pair of sites.
- **claim-common-word-moments-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-local-tightness)): The independent common-word variables satisfy $$1\le \bar L:=\mathbb E L\le p^{-2},\qquad \mathbb E\|S\|<\infty,\qquad \Pr(S\ne0)>0.$$ For a single conditioned walk, and for either marginal of a shared conditioned pair, the increments $Y_{h+j}-Y_h$ are tight uniformly in the deterministic level $h$ when $j$ ranges over any fixed bounded set of nonnegative integers.* *If $\sigma(H)$ is the first common true level at or above $H$ under $P_{x,y}^{++}$, then $$\sigma(H)-H,\quad Y^{(i)}_{\sigma(H)}-Y^{(i)}_H\ (i=1,2) \quad\text{are tight uniformly in }H,x,y. \tag{6}\label{eq:6}$$ At $\sigma(H)$ the pair suffix has the shared conditioned law from its new sites.
  - Explanation: A common word has mean width between the stated bounds and a finite first moment of its transverse increment, which is not almost surely zero. Increments over bounded level ranges and the first common level above a given level are tight uniformly.
- **claim-scale-function-maximal** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-scales)): The function $n$ is continuous and nondecreasing, tends to infinity, and is strictly increasing for all sufficiently large $r$. Write $r_s$ for its inverse on large scales, so $n(r_s)=s$. Then $$n(cr)\ge c^2n(r)\ (0<c\le1),\qquad n(r)\ge r/\mathbb E\|S\|. \tag{7}\label{eq:7}$$ For iid copies $S_j$, let $$M_H^S=\max_{0\le i\le H}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^iS_j\right\|.$$ Uniformly in integer $H\ge0$ and $z>0$, $$\mathbb E[(M_H^S)^2\wedge z^2]\le CHm(z),\qquad \Pr(M_H^S>z)\le CH/n(z). \tag{8}\label{eq:8}$$
  - Explanation: The truncated-variance scale function is continuous, nondecreasing, unbounded and eventually strictly increasing. It obeys scaling and linear lower bounds, and partial-sum maxima of the symmetric increments obey capped second-moment and tail bounds without a finite second moment.
- **claim-exact-level-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-exact-comparison)): For every integer $H\ge0$ and $z>0$, $$P_0^+(W_H>z)\le C\Pr(M_H^S>z). \tag{9}\label{eq:9}$$ No moment of the maximal decoration inside a common word is needed.
  - Explanation: The tail of the maximal centered displacement of a single conditioned walk is bounded by a constant times the tail of the maximal partial sum of common-word increments. No moment of decorations inside a word is used.
- **claim-median-linearization-short** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-median-linearization)): For sufficiently small $t>0$, uniformly in $1\le H\le tn(r)$, $$\max_{j\le H}\|b_j-jb_H/H\|\le C\sqrt t\,r. \tag{10}\label{eq:10}$$
  - Explanation: On short blocks the deterministic medians are within a small multiple of the scale of a linear interpolation.
- **claim-conditioning-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-conditioning-transfer)): Along any sequences of integers $H\ge0$ and real numbers $z\to\infty$, $$\limsup\widetilde P_0(W_H>z) \le C\limsup P_0^+(W_H>z/C) \tag{11}\label{eq:11}$$ with a fixed constant $C$. If also $H\to\infty$, the same bound holds with the left-hand side evaluated under the $P$-average of the normalized successful-prefix kernel $K_H/a_H$ at the origin.
  - Explanation: Large-deviation control of the maximal displacement under the conditioning that keeps the environment law transfers, up to a fixed constant, between the two forms of conditioning and to the averaged normalized kernel.
- **claim-short-block-survival** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-short-block)): For every $v_0>0$ there are constants $C_{v_0}<\infty$ and $t_{v_0}>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<t\le t_{v_0}$, some $\delta>0$ satisfies, for all sufficiently large $r$, every integer $1\le H\le tn(r)$, and every probability measure $\pi$ on a starting layer, $$P\left( \pi K_H\{W_H\le v_0r,\ \max_{j\le H}\|Y_j-jb_H/H\|\le v_0r\}<\delta \right) \le C_{v_0}t^2. \tag{12}\label{eq:12}$$ The environment is fresh with product law, and path displacements are measured from each path’s own starting site.
  - Explanation: On short blocks, the quenched kernel keeps a fixed positive mass inside a narrow median and slope tube, except on an environment event whose probability is a constant times the square of the block parameter.
- **claim-endpoint-spread** (bound, [source](#stmt-fluct-endpoint-spread)): Under the independent conditioned pair law, there is a constant $c>0$ such that $$\begin{split} &\Pr(\|\Delta_{\lfloor n(r)\rfloor}\|>cr)\ge c \qquad(r\ \text{large}),\\ &\liminf\Pr(\|\Delta_{\lfloor tn(r)\rfloor}\|>lr/4) \ge c_{l,\epsilon}t \quad\text{if }r\to\infty,\qquad n(r)\Pr(\|S\|>lr)\ge\epsilon. \end{split} \tag{13}\label{eq:13}$$ In the second assertion $0<l<1$ and $\epsilon>0$ are fixed, and the bound holds for each sufficiently small fixed $t>0$, depending on $l,\epsilon$. Relative to any deterministic endpoint center, a single $P^+$-walk consequently has a comparable probability of a deviation, at half the corresponding separation threshold, in at least one of the two signs.
  - Explanation: Two independent conditioned walks separate by a macroscopic amount at the truncated-variance scale with probability bounded below, and a similar lower bound holds at shorter heights when large jumps are not negligible. A single walk therefore deviates from any deterministic center in at least one sign with comparable probability.
- **claim-independent-brownian-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gauss-independent)): For two independent conditioned paths, $\Delta_h/r$ converges functionally to Brownian motion of variance parameter $1/\bar L$. For a single $P_0^+$ path, $(Y_h-b_h)/r$ converges functionally to Brownian motion of variance parameter $\sigma_*^2$.
  - Explanation: Along Gaussian sequences, the difference of two independent conditioned paths converges functionally to Brownian motion, and a single median-centered path converges functionally to Brownian motion with half that variance parameter.
- **claim-median-additivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-gauss-medians)): Uniformly for $k+j\le T n(r)$, for each fixed $T$, $$b_{k+j}-b_k-b_j=o(r). \tag{15}\label{eq:15}$$ Writing $N_r=\lfloor n(r)\rfloor$ and $\theta_r=b_{N_r}/N_r$, we have $$\max_{h\le Tn(r)}\|b_h-h\theta_r\|=o(r), \qquad \theta_r=o(r).$$
  - Explanation: The medians are additive up to a small error on the scale, and are globally approximated by a linear function over bounded multiples of the scale.
- **claim-shared-marginal-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gauss-shared-marginals)): Under $P_{x,y}^{++}$, each marginal process $$B_i^r(h/n(r))=(Y_h^{(i)}-h\theta_r)/r \tag{16}\label{eq:16}$$ converges functionally to Brownian motion of variance parameter $\sigma_*^2$, uniformly over starts on a common layer. Thus the conclusion holds along every choice $x=x(r),y=y(r)$ with $x_1=y_1$, whatever their separation. The pair is jointly tight.
  - Explanation: Each marginal of the shared-environment conditioned pair converges functionally to the same Brownian limit as a single walk, uniformly over starts on a common layer, and the pair is jointly tight.
- **claim-off-diagonal-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-gauss-off-diagonal)): Fix $a,t>0$. Uniformly over same-layer starts with $\|y_2-x_2\|\ge ar$, the total variation distance between the pair path-prefix laws through level $k=\lfloor tn(r)\rfloor$, under $P_{x,y}^{++}$ and under independent conditioned walks, satisfies $$\limsup {\rm dist}_{\rm TV}\le C\exp(-ca^2/t). \tag{17}\label{eq:17}$$ The constants depend only on the walk law.
  - Explanation: When the starts are separated at the scale, the shared and independent pair prefix laws are close in total variation, with an exponentially small error in the squared separation over the time parameter.
- **claim-vanishing-diagonal-occupation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gauss-diagonal)): Along every Gaussian sequence, under $P_{0,0}^{++}$, for each fixed finite $T$, $$\lim_{\rho\downarrow0}\limsup_{r\to\infty} \frac1{n(r)}E_{0,0}^{++} \sum_{0\le h\le Tn(r)}\mathbf1_{\{\|Z_h\|\le\rho r\}}=0. \tag{18}\label{eq:18}$$
  - Explanation: Along Gaussian sequences the shared pair spends asymptotically negligible normalized time with a small projected gap.
- **claim-shared-independent-joint-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gauss-joint)): Under $P_{0,0}^{++}$, the pair of processes in <a href="#eq:16" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:16">[eq:16]</a> converges functionally to two independent Brownian motions, each with variance parameter $\sigma_*^2$.
  - Explanation: The shared-environment pair converges functionally to two independent Brownian motions, each with the single-path variance parameter.
- **claim-quenched-gaussian-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gauss-quenched)): Along Gaussian sequences, the quenched path under $D$-conditioning from a fixed start has the Brownian limit of <a href="#eq:16" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:16">[eq:16]</a> in $P$-probability, in the sense of bounded continuous path tests. In particular, for every $\rho>0$ and every $b_*(r)\to\infty$, with $H=\lfloor n(r)\rfloor$, $$P\left(\frac{K_H(0,\{ W_H\le b_*(r)r,\ \|Y_H-b_H\|\le\rho r\})}{a_H} \ge c_\rho\right)\longrightarrow1 \tag{23}\label{eq:23}$$ for some $c_\rho>0$ depending only on $\rho$ and the walk law. The same assertion holds at translated starts.
  - Explanation: Along Gaussian sequences the quenched conditioned path has the Brownian limit in environment probability, and the normalized kernel keeps a fixed positive mass in a window around the median with probability tending to one.
- **claim-clipping-arbitrary-scales** (theorem, [source](#stmt-clip-clipping)): For any fixed $0<T<\infty,\ \eta>0,\ p_{\rm err}>0$ there is $d_0>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $r$, some deterministic slope $s_r$ satisfies, with $H_{\rm tot}=\lceil T n(r)\rceil$, $$P\left(K_{H_{\rm tot}}(x,\{\max_{j\le H_{\rm tot}}\|Y_j-j s_r\|\le\eta r\})\ge d_0\right)>1-p_{\rm err}. \tag{24}\label{eq:24}$$ The bound holds at every prescribed starting site $x$, with the same $d_0$ and slope. It also gives the corresponding lower bound at every shorter horizon by taking first-hit prefixes.
  - Explanation: At every large scale, with high environment probability, a fixed positive amount of quenched first-hit mass lies in a narrow tube around a deterministic line whose slope may depend on the scale but not on the environment or start.
- **claim-truncated-moment-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-clip-moment-transfer)): Write $\mathbb Q_h=E[K_h(0,\cdot)/a_h]$. For any $h\to\infty,\ z\to\infty$, put $X=W_h/z$ under $\mathbb Q_h$, and let $V=M_h^S/z$ have the iid sum law of <a href="#eq:8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:8">[eq:8]</a>. For fixed $0<A<L<\infty$, with a constant independent of $A,L$, $$\begin{split} \limsup \mathbb Q_h[(X\wedge L)^2]&\le C\limsup\mathbb E[(V\wedge L)^2],\\ \limsup \mathbb Q_h[(X\wedge L)^2\mathbf1_{X>A}]&\le C\limsup\mathbb E[(V\wedge L)^2\mathbf1_{V>A/C}]. \end{split} \tag{25}\label{eq:25}$$
  - Explanation: Truncated second moments of the maximal displacement under the averaged normalized kernel are controlled, up to a constant, by those of the independent sum maximum.
- **claim-initial-distribution-clipping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kernel-initial-clipping)): For fixed $k,T,\eta,p'>0$, with integer $k\ge2$, there is a positive constant $d$ such that, for every sufficiently large $r$, every $H\le Tn(r)$, and every deterministic initial probability $\pi$, $$P\big(\Gamma_H^\pi(\mathcal R_H(\eta r))\ge d\big)>1-p'.$$ The analogous single-walk clipping bound holds from any initial probability, with the deterministic slope supplied by <a href="#eq:24" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:24">[eq:24]</a>.
  - Explanation: For tuples of walks started from any deterministic initial probability, the relative displacement budget is respected by a fixed positive mass with high environment probability. The single-walk analogue also holds.
- **claim-separated-seeds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kernel-seeds)): For each fixed $k\ge2,\ p'>0$, there are constants $C_*,c_0,g_0>0$ such that for all sufficiently large integer $H$, putting $r=r_{H/C_*}$, $$P\big(\Gamma_H^\pi\{{\rm sep}(\text{endpoints})\ge c_0 r\}\ge g_0\big)>1-p' \tag{30}\label{eq:30}$$ from arbitrary $\pi$, without initial separation.
  - Explanation: From an arbitrary initial law without separation, after a height comparable to the scale, a fixed positive mass has endpoints separated at the scale, with high environment probability.
- **claim-separated-inverse-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-inverse-moment)): For each fixed positive $B_*<\infty$ and integer $k$, if initial separation on the support of $\pi$ is sufficiently large, depending on $B_*,k$, then $$P(q_\pi<2s)\le e^{C k}s^\lambda,\qquad s\ge e^{-B_*}, \tag{31}\label{eq:31}$$ with $C$ independent of $B_*,k$.
  - Explanation: When the initial sites are far apart, the product of survival probabilities has an inverse-power tail bound driven by the single-site negative moment.
- **claim-rapid-loss-unlikely** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-rapid-loss)): Suppose $\pi$ starts with ${\rm sep}\ge G$ and $0<d'\le G/2$, with $G-d'$ sufficiently large for <a href="#eq:31" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:31">[eq:31]</a>. For any positive integer $L'$ and $kL'\log(1/\kappa)\le x\le B_*$, the loss $Z=-\log\Gamma_H^\pi(\mathcal R_H(d'))$ satisfies $$P(Z>x)\le \left(e^{Ck-\lambda x/L'} + C k e^{x/L'}\,H/n(d'/(2L'))\right)^{L'}. \tag{32}\label{eq:32}$$ Here $C$ can be fixed independently of $k,L',B_*,H,d'$, given the stated separation condition.
  - Explanation: For separated tuples, a large logarithmic loss of mass while respecting the relative displacement budget is unlikely, with a bound obtained by subdividing the height into pieces.
- **claim-outward-order-polynomial** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernel-outward-order)): There are fixed $A_{\to},c>0$ and a nonnegative random function $\mathcal Z(x,y)$ of the upper environment such that, simultaneously in integer $H\ge1$, $$-\log p_H^\to(x,y)\le A_{\to}\log(1+H)+\mathcal Z(x,y),\qquad \sup_{x,y} E\exp(c\mathcal Z(x,y))<\infty. \tag{33}\label{eq:33}$$ Every cap $\mathcal Z\wedge B'$, $0<B'<\infty$, is approximable in $P$-probability uniformly in $x,y$ by bounded functions of fixed-radius upper row neighborhoods of these sites.
  - Explanation: The probability that two quenched paths do not decrease their signed gap is at worst polynomial in the height, up to a local random remainder with an exponential moment that is approximable by finitely many upper rows.
- **claim-separated-stages** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stage-prop)): There are fixed constants $f,g,\chi,K>0$ and an integer $k\ge2$ with $$\frac1g<\frac{\beta}{64},\qquad \frac{(1+f/g)D_0}{K}<\frac{\beta}{64},$$ having the following property. For every sufficiently large fixed $b$, put $B=kb$ and choose a sufficiently large fixed floor $s_{\rm fl}$. At every level-count scale $s\ge s_{\rm fl}$, put $$s_-=se^{-fb},\qquad s_+=se^{gb},\qquad H_e=\lfloor se^{-b}\rfloor,\qquad H=\lfloor se^{\chi b}\rfloor.$$ For every probability $\pi$ on $k$-tuples supported on $\{{\rm sep}\ge r_s\}$, a fresh product environment satisfies $$\begin{array}{ll} \Gamma_h^\pi\{{\rm sep}(\text{endpoints})\ge r_{s_-}\}\ge e^{-B} &(1\le h\le H),\\ \Gamma_H^\pi\{{\rm sep}(\text{endpoints})\ge r_{s_+}\}\ge e^{-B} \end{array} \tag{35}\label{eq:35}$$ with probability at least $1-e^{-2Kb}$. Each test uses the full kernel from the same stage base; a passing test does not restrict the paths used by later tests.
  - Explanation: Constants can be chosen so that, with probability close to one in a fresh environment, separated mass persists at all intermediate heights of a stage at a contracted scale and at the stage horizon at an expanded scale.
- **claim-episode-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stage-episodes)): For sufficiently large fixed $b$, the episode construction satisfies $M\le J+1$ and $$0\le c_i\le B_0/k+2bJ_i,\qquad \log(1+h_i)\le C(1+J_i). \tag{38}\label{eq:38}$$ For large $N$, $$J\le\frac{\log N}{gb}+(1+f/g)F+1. \tag{39}\label{eq:39}$$ Under $Q_N$, fixed constants $c,C>0$ and $L_0<\infty$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned} c\log N&\le E_{Q_N}M,& E_{Q_N}(M+J)&\le C\log N,\\ E_{Q_N}\sum_{i<M}c_i\mathbf1_{\{J_i\le L_0\}} &\ge c\log N.&& \end{aligned} \tag{40}\label{eq:40}$$ With the padding $t_i=N$ for $i\ge M$, each $t_i$ is a bounded layer stopping time, and $\{i<M\}$ is known at $t_i$.
  - Explanation: The episode construction has few episodes relative to stages, bounded loss and logarithmic height per episode, and under the bad-event law the expected episode count is of logarithmic order, with positive expected loss from episodes of bounded stage count.
- **claim-stationary-episode-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-stationary)): Along a subsequence, $\mathbb L_N$ converges weakly to a shift-invariant law $\mathbb L$. Under this law, simultaneously for every $i$, $$\begin{gathered} 1\le h_i<\infty,\qquad J_i,c_i<\infty,\\ E_{\mathbb L}\bigl(c_i+\log(1+h_i)\bigr)<\infty, \qquad E_{\mathbb L}c_i>0. \end{gathered}$$ Every stored row is uniformly elliptic with the original bound $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: Along a subsequence the episode-averaged laws converge weakly to a shift-invariant law under which marks are finite with finite logarithmic moment, the mean loss is positive and rows stay uniformly elliptic.
- **claim-finite-window-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-profile-entropy)): There is a constant $C_E<\infty$ such that, for every fixed $i$ and $m\ge1$, the $\mathbb L$-law of $(\mathcal A_i,(\Omega^{i,m,a})_a)$ has relative entropy at most $C_E m$ with respect to its own current-data marginal followed by the reference kernel $\mathbf R$.
  - Explanation: The joint law of current profile data and spliced upper environments over a window of episodes has relative entropy at most linear in the window length against its reference.
- **claim-sampling-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-sampling)): Under $\mathbb L$, simultaneously for all indices and horizontal $z$, $$2^{-n}\sum_{b\le2^n}\mathbf1_{\{O_{ia,jb}=z\}}\longrightarrow w_j^{ia}(z)\quad\text{a.s.} \tag{42}\label{eq:42}$$ A distinct label $(j,b)\ne(i,a)$ cannot have finite offset $z$ from $(i,a)$ while $w_j^{ia}(z)=0$.
  - Explanation: Empirical frequencies of sampled anchor offsets converge to the profile mass, and a distinct label cannot sit at a finite offset where the profile vanishes.
- **claim-profile-updates-survival** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-updates)): For every $i,m,a$, write $H=H_{i,m}$, $C=C_{i,m}$, and $\Omega=\Omega^{i,m,a}$. Coordinatewise, $$w_{i+m}^{ia}\ \ge\ e^C\, w_i^{ia} K_H^\Omega . \tag{43}\label{eq:43}$$ Here the kernel maps horizontal starting positions at layer zero to endpoints at layer $H$ in the anchor frame. Also, almost surely, $$\limsup_n 2^{-n}\sum_{a\le2^n} a_{H_{i,m}}(0;\Omega^{i,m,a})\le e^{-C_{i,m}}. \tag{44}\label{eq:44}$$
  - Explanation: The profile after several episodes dominates the earlier profile pushed through the local kernel and scaled by the accumulated normalization, and the anchor-averaged crossing probability is bounded by the exponential of minus that loss.
- **claim-finitely-many-profiles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-classes)): On $\mathcal E$, there is no dust. The proper classes form the same finite nonempty set at every time, and the profile of each class has full support on horizontal space.
  - Explanation: On the positive-loss event, there is no diffuse dust, and the proper profile classes form a fixed finite nonempty set at every time, each with full support.
- **claim-no-stationary-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-no-growth)): Suppose a stationary marked profile law has full-support subprobability profiles and nonnegative marks $c_i$, positive integer marks $h_i$, with $$E[c_0+\log(1+h_0)]<\infty.$$ Assume the coordinatewise update <a href="#eq:43" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:43">[eq:43]</a>, the finite entropy property <a href="#eq:45" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:45">[eq:45]</a> for every fixed $m$, and the outward-order estimate <a href="#eq:33" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:33">[eq:33]</a>, including its uniform local approximation property. Then $Ec_0=0$.
  - Explanation: A stationary marked profile law with the stated update, finite entropy and outward-order estimate cannot have a positive mean normalization loss, which contradicts the bad-event sequence.

```json
        "exact_quote": "The independent common-word variables satisfy $$1\\le \\bar L:=\\mathbb E L\\le p^{-2},\\qquad\n\\mathbb E|S|<\\infty,\\qquad \\Pr(S\\ne0)>0.$$ For a single conditioned walk, and for either marginal of a shared conditioned pair, the increments $Y_{h+j}-Y_h$ are tight uniformly in the deterministic level $h$ when $j$ ranges over any fixed bounded set of nonnegative integers.*\n\n\n*If $\\sigma(H)$ is the first common true level at or above $H$ under $P_{x,y}^{++}$, then $$\\sigma(H)-H,\\quad\nY^{(i)}_{\\sigma(H)}-Y^{(i)}_H\\ (i=1,2)\n\\quad\\text{are tight uniformly in }H,x,y.\n\\tag{6}\\label{eq:6}$$ At $\\sigma(H)$ the pair suffix has the shared conditioned law from its new sites.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fluct-short-block",
        "exact_quote": "For every $v_0>0$ there are constants $C_{v_0}<\\infty$ and $t_{v_0}>0$ such that, for each fixed $0<t\\le t_{v_0}$, some $\\delta>0$ satisfies, for all sufficiently large $r$, every integer $1\\le H\\le tn(r)$, and every probability measure $\\pi$ on a starting layer, $$P\\left(\n \\pi K_H\\{W_H\\le v_0r,\\\n       \\max_{j\\le H}|Y_j-jb_H/H|\\le v_0r\\}<\\delta\n \\right)\n \\le C_{v_0}t^2.\n\\tag{12}\\label{eq:12}$$ The environment is fresh with product law, and path displacements are measured from each path’s own starting site.",

          "source_id": "PM-220.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-fluct-endpoint-spread",
        "exact_quote": "Under the independent conditioned pair law, there is a constant $c>0$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n&\\Pr(|\\Delta_{\\lfloor n(r)\\rfloor}|>cr)\\ge c\n       \\qquad(r\\ \\text{large}),\\\\\n&\\liminf\\Pr(|\\Delta_{\\lfloor tn(r)\\rfloor}|>lr/4)\n       \\ge c_{l,\\epsilon}t\n \\quad\\text{if }r\\to\\infty,\\qquad\n       n(r)\\Pr(|S|>lr)\\ge\\epsilon.\n\\end{split}\n\\tag{13}\\label{eq:13}$$ In the second assertion $0<l<1$ and $\\epsilon>0$ are fixed, and the bound holds for each sufficiently small fixed $t>0$, depending on $l,\\epsilon$.         "anchor": "stmt-gauss-quenched",
        "exact_quote": "Along Gaussian sequences, the quenched path under $D$-conditioning from a fixed start has the Brownian limit of <a href=\"#eq:16\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:16\">[eq:16]</a> in $P$-probability, in the sense of bounded continuous path tests.         "anchor": "stmt-clip-moment-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Write $\\mathbb Q_h=E[K_h(0,\\cdot)/a_h]$. For fixed $0<A<L<\\infty$, with a constant independent of $A,L$, $$\\begin{split}\n\\limsup \\mathbb Q_h[(X\\wedge L)^2]&\\le C\\limsup\\mathbb E[(V\\wedge L)^2],\\\\\n\\limsup \\mathbb Q_h[(X\\wedge L)^2\\mathbf1_{X>A}]&\\le\n C\\limsup\\mathbb E[(V\\wedge L)^2\\mathbf1_{V>A/C}].\n\\end{split}\n\\tag{25}\\label{eq:25}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-220.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-kernel-rapid-loss",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\pi$ starts with ${\\rm sep}\\ge G$ and $0<d'\\le G/2$, with $G-d'$ sufficiently large for <a href=\"#eq:31\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:31\">[eq:31]</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **uniform ellipticity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Throughout, we assume *uniform ellipticity*: there is a constant $\kappa>0$ such that $$\nu\!\left(\min_{e\in U}p(e)\ge\kappa\right)=1.$$
- **directional transience and ballisticity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The walk is *directionally transient* in direction $\ell$ if $P_0(A_\ell)=1$, and *ballistic* in that direction if $$P_0\!\left(\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{X_n\cdot\ell}{n}>0\right)=1.$$
- **escape event** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a fixed $\ell\in S^{d-1}$, define $$A_\ell=\{X_n\cdot\ell\longrightarrow+\infty\}.$$
- **ordinary strict record** ([source](#pm-h-9)): An *ordinary strict record* is a first strict exceedance of the running height maximum, with time zero counted as the initial record.
- **true record** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Such a record is *true* if the walk never subsequently goes below its height.
- **layer hitting time** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For a path from $x$ and $j\in\mathbb Z$, $T_j=\inf\{n\ge0:(X_n)_1=x_1+j\}$ denotes first hit of the layer $x_1+j$, relative to the indicated start ($\inf\varnothing=\infty$; $T_0=0$).
- **survival and crossing probabilities** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Put $D=\{T_{-1}=\infty\}$, $p=P_x(D)>0$, $$P_x^+=P_x(\,\cdot\,\mid D),\qquad q(x)=P_{x,\omega}(D),\qquad a_H(x)=P_{x,\omega}(T_H<T_{-1})\quad(H\ge0\ \text{integer}),$$ where the environment in notation such as $q,a$ is implicit.
- **truncated variance scale** ([source](#pm-h-15)): For $r>0$, define $$m(r)=\mathbb E(S^2\wedge r^2),\qquad n(r)=r^2/m(r).$$
- **common true level** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A *common true level* is a level that is true for both walks at their respective first hits.
- **maximal centered displacement** ([source](#sec:fluctuations)): Choose a deterministic integer median $b_j$ of $Y_j$ under $P_0^+$, with $b_0=0$, and write $$W_H=\max_{0\le j\le H}\|Y_j-b_j\|.$$
- **Gaussian sequence** ([source](#stmt-gauss-sequence)): A sequence $r\to\infty$ is *Gaussian* if $$n(r)\Pr(\|S\|>a r)\longrightarrow0\qquad\text{for every fixed }a>0. \tag{14}\label{eq:14}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-ellipticity-constant | uniform ellipticity constant | > | $0$ | Deterministic constant fixed by the row law |
| q-mean-width-upper | mean height gain of a conditioned word | ≤ | $p_h^{-1}$ | Fixed row law and direction |
| q-common-word-mean-upper | mean common-word width | ≤ | $p^{-2}$ | Fixed row law |
| q-scale-compression | scale function under compression | ≥ | $c^2n(r)$ | Large scales |
| q-scale-linear-lower | scale function lower bound | ≥ | $r/\mathbb E\|S\|$ | All scales |
| q-partial-sum-tail | tail of the maximal partial sum of common-word increments | ≤ | $CH/n(z)$ | Constant C depends only on the walk law |
| q-median-linearization-error | median linearization error | ≤ | $C\sqrt t\,r$ | Small block parameter |
| q-short-block-failure | probability of losing kernel mass in the slope tube | ≤ | $C_{v_0}t^2$ | Block parameter at most the stated threshold |
| q-off-diagonal-tv | total variation between shared and independent pair prefixes | ≤ | $C\exp(-ca^2/t)$ | Limsup in the scale parameter |
| q-inverse-moment-tail | lower tail of the product of survival probabilities | ≤ | $e^{C k}s^\lambda$ | Thresholds not below the exponential of minus the cap |
| q-outward-order-cost | negative log of the outward-order mass | ≤ | $A_{\to}\log(1+H)+\mathcal Z(x,y)$ | Remainder with a uniform exponential moment |
| q-stage-success-probability | probability that the stage separation tests pass | ≥ | $1-e^{-2Kb}$ | Constants fixed before the stage parameter |
| q-stage-growth-rate | inverse stage growth exponent | < | $\frac{\beta}{64}$ | Exponent fixed in the contradiction argument |
| q-episodes-vs-stages | number of episodes | ≤ | $J+1$ | Total number of stages J |
| q-expected-episodes-lower | expected number of episodes under the bad-event law | ≥ | $c\log N$ | Large N |
| q-window-entropy | relative entropy over a window of episodes | ≤ | $C_E m$ | Window length |
| q-stationary-loss-zero | mean normalization loss of a stationary marked profile law | = | $0$ | Conclusion forcing a contradiction |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-true-words** [claim](#stmt-geom-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-words): Minimum argument for positivity of the non-backtracking probability, fresh-row factorization at true records, renewal counting of record indices. -> Independent identical words between true boundaries with finite mean width.
- **step-radius** [claim](#stmt-geom-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-radius): Contradiction with an infinite mean radius using a maximal ergodic inequality, a polynomial lower bound for large displacement, and a fresh forward continuation that caps the ratio. -> Finite mean spatial radius of a word.
- **step-ray** [claim](#stmt-geom-ray) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-ray): Strong law on word endpoints plus summable radius tails, then relabeling a positive signed coordinate as height. -> Deterministic limiting ray and coordinate height.
- **step-inverse-moment** [claim](#stmt-geom-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-inverse): Tail zero-one law for positivity, then iteration over stopping layers with a conditional Markov bound. -> Positive survival probability and a small inverse moment.
- **step-speed** [claim](#stmt-geom-speed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-speed): Confinement of the path before strip exit, super-polynomial width tails by a renewal recurrence, geometric visit-count bound, then renewal law of large numbers. -> Finite mean duration and positive deterministic velocity.
- **step-common-words** [claim](#stmt-fluct-common-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-common-words): Test fresh levels above both explored pasts, enumerate admissible word pairs and factor path weights from suffix probabilities. -> Common true levels and fresh continuation laws.
- **step-local-tightness** [claim](#stmt-fluct-local-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-local-tightness): Renewal mass for common words, covering-word estimate, and repeated tests for visible failure. -> Moment bounds and uniform tightness of local increments.
- **step-scales** [claim](#stmt-fluct-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-scales): Monotonicity of the truncated second moment ratio and symmetric truncation with a square maximal inequality. -> Scale function and maximal bounds.
- **step-exact-comparison** [claim](#stmt-fluct-exact-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-exact-comparison): Select the first offending level, add an independent opposite-side walk, impose fresh truth at both endpoints. -> Single-walk maximal tail dominated by the common-word maximal tail.
- **step-median-linearization** [claim](#stmt-fluct-median-linearization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-median-linearization): Approximate additivity of medians on exactly true levels, then a doubling recursion for the deviation from the line. -> Linear approximation of medians on short blocks.
- **step-conditioning-transfer** [claim](#stmt-fluct-conditioning-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-conditioning-transfer): Approximate the density between the two conditionings by a finite-row function and remove the initial segment using a true boundary. -> Transfer of fluctuation bounds between conditionings.
- **step-short-block** [claim](#stmt-fluct-short-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-short-block): One possible loss of mass, a fresh restart after the first failure, and concatenation of retained submeasures. -> Short-block survival bound.
- **step-endpoint-spread** [claim](#stmt-fluct-endpoint-spread) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fluct-endpoint-spread): Approximate the pair difference by a sum of common-word increments and apply second-moment and sign arguments. -> Spread of endpoints at the scale.
- **step-independent-limit** [claim](#stmt-gauss-independent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-independent): Truncated-sum Brownian limit at common cuts, passage to every first hit, and recovery of a single path by a normal decomposition argument. -> Brownian limits for pair differences and single paths.
- **step-medians** [claim](#stmt-gauss-medians) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-medians): Domination of conditional by unconditional laws forces zero shift, then the doubling recursion. -> Additive and linear median behavior.
- **step-shared-marginals** [claim](#stmt-gauss-shared-marginals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-shared-marginals): Replace the survival weight of the second walk by a finite-layer weight and factor at a true boundary. -> Brownian marginals under the shared law.
- **step-off-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-gauss-off-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-off-diagonal): Bound trace contact by endpoint concentration in disjoint windows and compare raw prefix weights off contact. -> Total variation decoupling for separated starts.
- **step-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-gauss-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-diagonal): Capped power potential with drift at good radii, a cutoff above exceptional radii, an escape argument, and counting of common cuts. -> Vanishing normalized diagonal occupation.
- **step-joint-limit** [claim](#stmt-gauss-joint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-joint): Conditional marginal increments, off-diagonal cross increments, and a characteristic-function identification of the joint law. -> Independent Brownian limit for the shared pair.
- **step-quenched** [claim](#stmt-gauss-quenched) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauss-quenched): Second-moment method under a squared-survival reweighting of the environment. -> Quenched Brownian limit in probability and positive window mass.
- **step-moment-transfer** [claim](#stmt-clip-moment-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-moment-transfer): Compactified weak limits of the two laws and tail comparison. -> Truncated moment transfer.
- **step-clipping** [claim](#stmt-clip-clipping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-clip-moment-transfer-2): Concatenate short blocks with an auxiliary sampling rule, in a broad restriction case and a Gaussian subscale case, then transfer to genuine mass. -> Positive mass in a narrow tube at every scale.
- **step-initial-clipping** [claim](#stmt-kernel-initial-clipping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernel-initial-clipping): Sitewise clipping with a union bound and a Markov argument on the bad fraction of tuples. -> Clipping from an arbitrary initial distribution.
- **step-seeds** [claim](#stmt-kernel-seeds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernel-seeds): Split the mass into groups by designated deviations with central preservation, then choose endpoints successively. -> Separated endpoint seeds.
- **step-separated-inverse-moment** [claim](#stmt-kernel-inverse-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernel-inverse-moment): Truncate upward, use product-field mixing at separated sites, then Jensen and Markov. -> Inverse-moment bound for separated products.
- **step-rapid-loss** [claim](#stmt-kernel-rapid-loss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernel-rapid-loss): Monitor mass with a budget on subdivided pieces, extend by upward steps at a threat, and multiply conditional threat bounds. -> Rapid loss is unlikely.
- **step-outward-order** [claim](#stmt-kernel-outward-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernel-outward-order): Stage construction that grows or shrinks a buffer between walks, with a supermartingale bound on the number of stages. -> Polynomial cost for outward order with an exponentially integrable remainder.
- **step-stage-prop** [claim](#stmt-stage-prop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stage-prop): Nested schedule of opportunities per target cell, protected seeds with cumulative cost tests, union bound over cells, ordered choice of constants. -> Separated stage estimates with a small failure probability.
- **step-episodes** [claim](#stmt-stage-episodes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stage-episodes): Mass and height accounting inside an episode, counting scale changes, and transferring the failure bound to the bad-event law by the entropy inequality. -> Episode bounds.
- **step-profile-stationary** [claim](#stmt-profile-stationary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-stationary): Compactness of shift-averaged laws and telescoping of the occupation sum. -> Stationary episode limit with positive mean loss.
- **step-profile-entropy** [claim](#stmt-profile-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-entropy): Variational formula for relative entropy, conditional Jensen on stopped densities and lower semicontinuity in the limit. -> Finite-window entropy bound.
- **step-profile-sampling** [claim](#stmt-profile-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-sampling): Mean-square error of conditionally independent anchor draws and Borel–Cantelli. -> Sampling identities.
- **step-profile-updates** [claim](#stmt-profile-updates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-updates): Global survival mass inequality before the limit, then passage through finite-path restrictions and a Chebyshev bound. -> Profile update inequality and averaged survival bound.
- **step-profile-classes** [claim](#stmt-profile-classes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-classes): Exclude dust by a frequency argument, bound the number of proper classes by entropy and the inverse moment, and prove persistence and full support. -> Finitely many persistent full-support profile classes.
- **step-no-growth** [claim](#stmt-profile-no-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-no-growth): Two-tail statistic, outward-order transport of the tails, removal of environmental bias in the remainder with finite-row approximations, then a logarithmic moment bound as the window grows. -> Zero mean normalization loss for a stationary marked profile law.
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-no-growth-2): The no-growth proposition contradicts the positive loss on the bad-event sequence, giving the crossing estimate, and the speed reduction then gives the velocity along the ray. -> Directional transience implies ballisticity.
- **step-positive-transience** [claim](#stmt-cor-positive-transience) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-positive-transience): Upgrade positive transience probability by the companion zero-one law, apply the main theorem, then use uniqueness of the vector limit for the direction sets. -> Velocity hemisphere characterization.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-transience-implies-velocity**: For nearest-neighbor walks in iid uniformly elliptic environments in dimension at least two, almost-sure transience in one fixed direction forces a deterministic limiting velocity with strictly positive projection on that direction.
- **inv-crossing-bound-gives-speed**: The super-polynomial bound on small quenched crossing probabilities, for one fixed exponent in the open unit interval, yields finite mean regeneration duration and a positive deterministic speed.
- **inv-positive-transience-hemisphere**: In dimension at least three, positive transience probability in a fixed direction upgrades to a deterministic velocity, and the directions with positive escape probability form the open hemisphere of the velocity.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-one-dimension**: Apply the ballisticity conclusion to a one-dimensional walk. -> A transient walk can have zero speed, so no positive velocity follows.
- **anti-non-elliptic**: Apply the theorem to a network with blocked or zero-probability transitions. -> Traps can slow the walk even if it escapes; the argument relies on a uniform positive lower bound on every transition.
- **anti-correlated-media**: Apply the theorem to an environment whose sites are spatially correlated. -> The fresh-row factorization behind the regeneration words and renewal arguments breaks down.
- **anti-simultaneous-directions**: Read the theorem as one null set that works for all directions at once. -> The statement is annealed and per fixed direction; a pathwise claim over all directions is not given.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic almost-sure statements about infinite random walks in random environments, proved by contradiction with limits of stationary laws; the paper gives no finite procedure with explicit steps that outputs a computable object, so no runnable reference with an independent oracle exists.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a limit theorem about walks on the integer lattice and gives no procedure that a program could run. It offers no input/output contract for library code, only an almost-sure statement about a stochastic process. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem could serve as a sanity benchmark for a lattice random-walk simulator, since the walk is asserted to have a positive deterministic speed. It does not give a simulation method, and the velocity is not computable from it. The paper prescribes no sampling procedure. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker, estimator or analysis tool is defined. The proof is an existence argument built from stopping layers, entropy bounds and limits, and nothing in it is a deliverable tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument is nonconstructive. The proof is by contradiction against a bad-event sequence of environment laws, so it yields no finite procedure with a running time. The regeneration-word decomposition is a probabilistic structure, not an algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced. The environments, kernels and profile arrays in the paper are analytic objects in compact product spaces, not representations meant to be stored or queried. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no arithmetic kernel, no fixed-width computation and no throughput question. Its estimates are asymptotic in the scale and give no hardware-relevant constants. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no adversary, hardness assumption or security notion. The random environment is a modelling object for transport, not a source of cryptographic randomness. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or generalisation claim appears. The entropy tools are used only to compare environment laws inside the proof, not to train or evaluate a model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective function, feasible set or optimisation procedure. The theorem asserts the existence of a limiting velocity and does not optimise anything. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Walkers in a shared environment are a proof device for second-moment comparisons. They are not a model of communicating processes, and the paper has no protocol, fault model or consistency question. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is probability theory, as a proof that directional escape forces linear speed. It is a pure theorem about random media, and it yields no engineering artefact with a construction that could be tested. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dimension-at-least-two** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated only for dimension at least two. The paper itself notes that in one dimension a transient walk in an iid uniformly elliptic environment can have zero speed, so no positive velocity can be claimed there. Blocked use: ['Applying the ballisticity conclusion to a one-dimensional walk', 'Using the theorem as a speed guarantee for linear state chains']
- **limit-uniform-ellipticity** (hypothesis): A fixed positive lower bound on every nearest-neighbor transition probability is assumed throughout. Without it, blocked or zero-probability transitions can create traps that slow an escaping walk. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to percolation clusters or networks with broken links', 'Applying the theorem to media where some transition rows have zero entries']
- **limit-iid-product-law** (hypothesis): The environment is a product law, with independent and identically distributed rows at different sites. The regeneration factorization and the second-moment comparisons rely on independence over sites. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to spatially correlated random media', 'Applying the theorem to environments with long-range dependence between rows']
- **limit-fixed-direction-annealed** (formal_scope): The conclusion is for each fixed direction and is an annealed almost-sure statement. It does not provide one exceptional null set valid for all directions simultaneously. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as a simultaneous pathwise statement over all directions', 'Choosing the direction after seeing the environment or the path']
- **limit-positive-transience-dimension-three** (hypothesis): The upgrade from positive escape probability to a deterministic velocity is stated only for dimension at least three, because it rests on the companion zero-one law. Blocked use: ['Assuming positive transience probability suffices in dimension two', 'Using the velocity hemisphere characterization in dimension two']
- **limit-speed-reduction-conditional** (hypothesis): The reduction from crossing probabilities to finite mean duration and positive speed is conditional on the super-polynomial crossing estimate for one fixed exponent. It is not a free-standing result. Blocked use: ['Quoting finite mean regeneration duration without the crossing estimate', 'Using the speed reduction for walks where the crossing estimate is unknown']
- **limit-gaussian-scales-only** (edge_case): The Brownian and quenched limits are proved only along Gaussian sequences, where large jumps of the common-word increment are negligible. At other scales only the weaker clipping conclusion is available. Blocked use: ['Claiming a quenched invariance principle at every scale', 'Assuming a finite second moment of the increment']
- **limit-nonconstructive-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The crossing estimate is proved by contradiction against a bad-event sequence, using a subsequential weak limit by compactness. Constants and the deterministic slopes are shown to exist, not computed. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or explicit constants for the velocity', 'Deriving effective rates of convergence from the argument']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 220 (`lean/docs/220.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A directional zero–one law under strict ellipticity](../../preprints/A-directional-zero-one-law-under-strict-ellipticity-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Directional transience implies ballisticity](../../preprints/Directional-transience-implies-ballisticity-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The directional zero–one conjecture asks whether a random walk's probability of escape in a fixed direction must be zero or one. The formalization proves this for nearest-neighbor walks in independent identically distributed strictly elliptic environments on $\mathbb Z^d$, for every $d\ge3$ and every nonzero real direction. Strict ellipticity means that every allowed transition probability is positive almost surely; no uniform positive lower bound or moment condition is assumed. The probability is the annealed law from the origin.
>
> For an i.i.d. uniformly elliptic nearest-neighbor random environment on $\mathbb Z^d$, the formalization proves that almost-sure directional transience implies convergence of $X_n/n$ to a deterministic velocity with positive projection in that direction for every $d\ge2$.
>
> For $d\ge3$, positive probability of transience in any nonzero direction already suffices. The limiting velocity is unique, and the unit directions with positive transience probability are exactly the open hemisphere having positive inner product with that velocity; transience has probability one on that hemisphere and zero on its complement. The environment is sampled once and retained along the walk, and the probabilities use the annealed law from the origin.

Comparator source `DirectionalBallisticity.lean` (3946 bytes, sha256 `f1225d29e53266aa298b6f09d2b4d9b9f54122199142d1f58ac1b082c0b441c4`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter
open scoped ENNReal NNReal BigOperators Topology

namespace DirectionalTransience

abbrev Lattice (d : ℕ) := Fin d → ℤ
abbrev Direction (d : ℕ) := Fin d × Bool
abbrev Vector (d : ℕ) := Fin d → ℝ
abbrev Row (d : ℕ) := {p : Direction d → ℝ≥0 // ∑ e, p e = 1}
abbrev Environment (d : ℕ) := Lattice d → Row d
abbrev State (d : ℕ) := Environment d × Lattice d
abbrev Path (d : ℕ) := ℕ → Lattice d

def step {d : ℕ} (e : Direction d) : Lattice d :=
  fun i => if i = e.1 then (if e.2 then 1 else -1) else 0

noncomputable def environmentLaw {d : ℕ} (ν : Measure (Row d)) :
    Measure (Environment d) := Measure.infinitePi (fun _ => ν)

def UniformElliptic {d : ℕ} (ν : Measure (Row d)) : Prop :=
  ∃ κ : ℝ≥0, 0 < κ ∧ ∀ᵐ p ∂ν, ∀ e, κ ≤ p.1 e

lemma measurable_current_row {d : ℕ} :
    Measurable (fun z : State d => z.1 z.2) := by
  apply measurable_from_prod_countable_left
  intro x
  exact measurable_pi_apply x

noncomputable def transition {d : ℕ} : Kernel (State d) (State d) where
  toFun z := ∑ e : Direction d,
    ((z.1 z.2).1 e : ℝ≥0∞) • Measure.dirac (z.1, z.2 + step e)
  measurable' := by
    apply Measure.measurable_of_measurable_coe
    intro s hs
    simp only [Measure.finsetSum_apply, Measure.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul]
    apply Finset.measurable_fun_sum
    intro e _
    apply Measurable.mul
    · exact (measurable_pi_apply e |>.comp
        (measurable_subtype_coe.comp measurable_current_row)).coe_nnreal_ennreal
    · exact (Measure.measurable_coe hs).comp
        (Measure.measurable_dirac.comp (measurable_fst.prodMk
          (measurable_snd.add_const (step e))))

instance transition_markov {d : ℕ} : IsMarkovKernel (transition (d := d)) where
  isProbabilityMeasure z := by
    constructor
    change (∑ e : Direction d,
      ((z.1 z.2).1 e : ℝ≥0∞) • Measure.dirac (z.1, z.2 + step e)) Set.univ = 1
    simp only [Measure.finsetSum_apply, Measure.smul_apply, Measure.dirac_apply_of_mem
      (Set.mem_univ _), smul_eq_mul, mul_one]
    rw [← ENNReal.ofNNReal_finsetSum, (z.1 z.2).2]
    rfl

noncomputable def historyTransition {d : ℕ} (n : ℕ) :
    Kernel ((i : Finset.Iic n) → State d) (State d) where
  toFun z := transition (z ⟨n, Finset.mem_Iic.mpr le_rfl⟩)
  measurable' := transition.measurable.comp (measurable_pi_apply _)

instance historyTransition_markov {d : ℕ} (n : ℕ) :
    IsMarkovKernel (historyTransition (d := d) n) where
  isProbabilityMeasure _ := transition_markov.isProbabilityMeasure _

noncomputable def initialLaw {d : ℕ} (ν : Measure (Row d)) : Measure (State d) :=
  (environmentLaw ν).map (fun ω => (ω, (0 : Lattice d)))

noncomputable def annealedLaw {d : ℕ} (ν : Measure (Row d)) : Measure (Path d) :=
  (@Kernel.trajMeasure (fun _ => State d) _ (initialLaw ν) (historyTransition (d := d)) _).map
    (fun z n => (z n).2)

def dot {d : ℕ} (v ℓ : Vector d) : ℝ := ∑ i, v i * ℓ i

def realPosition {d : ℕ} (x : Lattice d) : Vector d := fun i => (x i : ℝ)

def DirectionallyTransient {d : ℕ} (ν : Measure (Row d)) (ℓ : Vector d) : Prop :=
  ∀ᵐ X ∂annealedLaw ν,
    Tendsto (fun n => dot (realPosition (X n)) ℓ) atTop atTop

def HasVelocity {d : ℕ} (ν : Measure (Row d)) (v : Vector d) : Prop :=
  ∀ᵐ X ∂annealedLaw ν,
    Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => (fun i => (X n i : ℝ) / (n : ℝ))) atTop (𝓝 v)

open scoped ENNReal NNReal Classical Topology BigOperators

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ (d : ℕ), 2 ≤ d → ∀ (ν : Measure (Row d)), IsProbabilityMeasure ν →
    UniformElliptic ν → ∀ (ℓ : Vector d), dot ℓ ℓ = 1 →
    DirectionallyTransient ν ℓ →
    ∃ v : Vector d, 0 < dot v ℓ ∧ HasVelocity ν v

theorem directional_transience_implies_ballisticity : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end DirectionalTransience

end OAI
```

```json
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [A directional zero–one law under strict ellipticity](../../preprints/A-directional-zero-one-law-under-strict-ellipticity-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Directional transience implies ballisticity](../../preprints/Directional-transience-implies-ballisticity-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe directional zero–one conjecture asks whether a random walk's probability of escape in a fixed direction must be zero or one. The formalization proves this for nearest-neighbor walks in independent identically distributed strictly elliptic environments on $\\mathbb Z^d$, for every $d\\ge3$ and every nonzero real direction. Strict ellipticity means that every allowed transition probability is positive almost surely; no uniform positive lower bound or moment condition is assumed. The probability is the annealed law from the origin.\n\nFor an i.i.d. uniformly elliptic nearest-neighbor random environment on $\\mathbb Z^d$, the formalization proves that almost-sure directional transience implies convergence of $X_n/n$ to a deterministic velocity with positive projection in that direction for every $d\\ge2$.\n\nFor $d\\ge3$, positive probability of transience in any nonzero direction already suffices. The limiting velocity is unique, and the unit directions with positive transience probability are exactly the open hemisphere having positive inner product with that velocity; transience has probability one on that hemisphere and zero on its complement. The environment is sampled once and retained along the walk, and the probabilities use the annealed law from the origin."
  },
  "status": "present",
  "toolchain": "leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1"
}
```
<!-- PM:END pm.formal.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that almost-sure transience in a fixed direction implies a deterministic limiting velocity with positive projection in that direction for nearest-neighbor random walks in independent and identically distributed uniformly elliptic environments on ℤ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, <i>d</i> ≥ 2. This resolves the ballisticity conjecture positively.
